					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Prime Minister of India from 1989 to 1990</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Not to be confused with politician <a href="/wiki/V._P._Singh_Badnore" title="V. P. Singh Badnore">V. P. Singh Badnore</a>, Minister and former General <a href="/wiki/V._K._Singh" title="V. K. Singh">V. K. Singh</a>, or hydrologist <a href="/wiki/Vijay_P._Singh" title="Vijay P. Singh">Vijay P. Singh</a>.</div>

class="mw-redirect" title="LL.B.">LL.B.</a>)<br /><a href="/wiki/University_of_Pune" class="mw-redirect" title="University of Pune">University of Pune</a> (<a href="/wiki/Bachelor_of_Science" </td></tr><tr style="display:none"><td colspan="2">
</td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below" style="border-top: 1px solid right; font-size: 95%"><div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Vishwanath Pratap Singh</b> (25 June 1931 – 27 November 2008), shortened to <b>V. P. Singh</b>, was an <a href="/wiki/Indian_politician" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian politician">Indian politician</a> who was the <a href="/wiki/List_of_prime_ministers_of_India" title="List of prime ministers of India">7th</a> <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">Prime Minister of India</a> from 1989 to 1990<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> and the 41st <a href="/wiki/Raja_Bahadur" class="mw-redirect" title="Raja Bahadur">Raja Bahadur</a> of <a href="/wiki/Manda_(zamindari)" title="Manda (zamindari)">Manda</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> He is India's only prime minister to have been former royalty.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>He was educated at the <a href="/wiki/Allahabad_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Allahabad University">Allahabad University</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fergusson_College" title="Fergusson College">Fergusson College</a> in Pune.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>  In 1969, he joined the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a> party and was elected as a member of the <a href="/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh_Legislative_Assembly" title="Uttar Pradesh Legislative Assembly">Uttar Pradesh Legislative Assembly</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> In 1971, he became a <a href="/wiki/Member_of_Parliament_(India)" title="Member of Parliament (India)">Member of Parliament</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Lok_Sabha" title="Lok Sabha">Lok Sabha</a>. He served as the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Commerce_and_Industry_(India)" title="Ministry of Commerce and Industry (India)">Minister of Commerce</a> from 1976 to 1977.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>  In 1980, he became the <a href="/wiki/List_of_chief_ministers_of_Uttar_Pradesh" title="List of chief ministers of Uttar Pradesh">Chief Minister of Uttar Pradesh</a> and was known for the encounter of the gang of <a href="/wiki/Phoolan_Devi" title="Phoolan Devi">Phoolan Devi</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In the <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi_ministry" class="mw-redirect" title="Rajiv Gandhi ministry">Rajiv Gandhi ministry</a>, Singh was given various cabinet posts, including <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Finance_(India)" title="Minister of Finance (India)">Minister of Finance</a> and <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Defence_(India)" title="Minister of Defence (India)">Minister of Defence</a>. Singh was also the <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_House_(Rajya_Sabha)" class="mw-redirect" title="Leader of the House (Rajya Sabha)">Leader of the Rajya Sabha</a> from 1984 to 1987. During his tenure as Minister of Defence, the <a href="/wiki/Bofors_scandal" title="Bofors scandal">Bofors scandal</a> came to light, and Singh resigned from the ministry. In 1988, he formed the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a> party by merging various factions of the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a>. In the <a href="/wiki/1989_Indian_general_election" title="1989 Indian general election">1989 elections</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Third_Front_(India)" title="Third Front (India)">National Front</a>, with the support of the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> (BJP), formed the government and Singh became the 8th Prime Minister of India.
</p><p>During his tenure as prime minister, he implemented the <a href="/wiki/Mandal_Commission" title="Mandal Commission">Mandal Commission</a> report for India's <a href="/wiki/Other_Backward_Class" title="Other Backward Class">backward castes</a>, which led to major <a href="/wiki/Mandal_Commission_protests_of_1990" title="Mandal Commission protests of 1990">protests</a> against the act. He also created the <a href="/wiki/Sixty-second_Amendment_of_the_Constitution_of_India" title="Sixty-second Amendment of the Constitution of India">Sixty-second Amendment</a> and enacted the <a href="/wiki/Scheduled_Caste_and_Scheduled_Tribe_(Prevention_of_Atrocities)_Act,_1989" title="Scheduled Caste and Scheduled Tribe (Prevention of Atrocities) Act, 1989">Scheduled Caste and Scheduled Tribe Act</a> in 1989. During his term the <a href="/wiki/1989_kidnapping_of_Rubaiya_Sayeed" class="mw-redirect" title="1989 kidnapping of Rubaiya Sayeed">kidnapping of Rubaiya Sayeed</a> happened and on the ground the terrorists were released. In 1990 the infamous <a href="/wiki/Exodus_of_Kashmiri_Hindus" title="Exodus of Kashmiri Hindus">exodus of Kashmiri Hindus</a> happened from the valley of <a href="/wiki/Kashmir_Valley" title="Kashmir Valley">Kashmir</a>. Following his opposition to the <a href="/wiki/Ram_Rath_Yatra" title="Ram Rath Yatra">Ram Rath Yatra</a>, the BJP withdrew its support for the National Front, and his government lost the <a href="/wiki/Motion_of_no_confidence" title="Motion of no confidence">vote of no-confidence</a>. Singh resigned on 7 November 1990. His prime ministerial tenure lasted for 343 days.
</p><p>Singh was the prime ministerial candidate for the <a href="/wiki/Third_Front_(India)" title="Third Front (India)">National Front</a> in the <a href="/wiki/1991_Indian_general_election" title="1991 Indian general election">1991 elections</a>, but was defeated. He spoke out against the <a href="/wiki/Demolition_of_the_Babri_Masjid" title="Demolition of the Babri Masjid">Babri Masjid demolition</a> in 1992. After 1996, Singh retired from political posts, but continued to remain a public figure and political critic. He was diagnosed with <a href="/wiki/Multiple_myeloma" title="Multiple myeloma">multiple myeloma</a> in 1998, and ceased public appearances until the cancer went into remission in 2003. However, he died from complications of multiple myeloma and kidney failure in 2008. He received full state honours.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life_and_education">Early life and education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life and education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Singh was born on 25 June 1931,<sup id="cite_ref-NYT_Obituary_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYT_Obituary-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> the third child of the <a href="/wiki/Hindu" class="mw-redirect" title="Hindu">Hindu</a> <a href="/wiki/Rajput" title="Rajput">Rajput</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Zamindar" title="Zamindar">Zamindar</a></i> family<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> of Daiya, which is located on the banks of the <a href="/wiki/Belan_River" title="Belan River">Belan River</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Allahabad_district" class="mw-redirect" title="Allahabad district">Allahabad district</a>. He was adopted by Raja Bahadur Ram Gopal Singh of <a href="/wiki/Manda_(zamindari)" title="Manda (zamindari)">Manda</a> and became the heir-apparent. He became the <a href="/wiki/Raja_Bahadur" class="mw-redirect" title="Raja Bahadur">Raja Bahadur</a> of <a href="/wiki/Manda_(zamindari)" title="Manda (zamindari)">Manda</a> at the age of 10 in 1941.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> His ancestors were rulers of the predecessor state of <a href="/wiki/Manikpur,_Uttar_Pradesh" class="mw-redirect" title="Manikpur, Uttar Pradesh">Manikpur</a> was founded in 1180, by Raja Manik Chand, brother of <a href="/wiki/Jayachandra" title="Jayachandra">Raja Jai Chand</a> of <a href="/wiki/Kannauj" title="Kannauj">Kannauj</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;A&#93;</a></sup> His family belonged to the <a href="/wiki/Gaharwar" class="mw-redirect" title="Gaharwar">Gaharwar</a> clan of the <a href="/wiki/Manda_(zamindari)" title="Manda (zamindari)">Manda</a> <a href="/wiki/Zamindar" title="Zamindar">Zamindar</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He obtained his education from <a href="/wiki/Colonel_Brown_Cambridge_School" title="Colonel Brown Cambridge School">Colonel Brown Cambridge School</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dehradun" title="Dehradun">Dehradun</a>, and got his Bachelor of Arts and <a href="/wiki/Bachelor_of_Laws" title="Bachelor of Laws">Law</a> degree from <a href="/wiki/Allahabad_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Allahabad University">Allahabad University</a>. He was elected the vice president of <a href="/wiki/Allahabad_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Allahabad University">Allahabad University</a> Students Union and later received a Bachelor of Science in physics from <a href="/wiki/Fergusson_College" title="Fergusson College">Fergusson College</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Savitribai_Phule_Pune_University" title="Savitribai Phule Pune University">Pune University</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Suri1990_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Suri1990-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_political_career">Early political career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early political career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Singh was elected from <a href="/wiki/Soraon_(Assembly_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Soraon (Assembly constituency)">Soraon</a><sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> to the Uttar Pradesh Legislative Assembly in 1969 as a member of the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Congress Party</a> and became the <a href="/wiki/Whip_(politics)" title="Whip (politics)">chief whip</a> for the legislative party. He got elected to the Lok Sabha in 1971 and was appointed a Deputy Minister of Commerce by Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a> in 1974. He served as the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Commerce_and_Industry_(India)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister of Commerce and Industry (India)">Minister of Commerce</a> in 1976–77.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Chief_Minister_of_Uttar_Pradesh">Chief Minister of Uttar Pradesh</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Chief Minister of Uttar Pradesh">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>He was appointed as the Chief Minister of Uttar Pradesh in 1980 when Indira Gandhi was re-elected after the Janata interlude.<sup id="cite_ref-Britannica_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Britannica-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> As Chief Minister (1980–82), he cracked down hard on <a href="/wiki/Dacoity" title="Dacoity">dacoity</a>, a problem that was particularly severe in the rural districts of the southwest Uttar Pradesh. He received much favourable national publicity when he offered to resign following a self-professed failure to stamp out the problem, and again when he personally oversaw the surrender of some of the most feared dacoits of the area in 1983.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> The Behmai massacre provoked outrage across the country thereby causing V. P. Singh to resign in the wake of the killings.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> as he was the under whom <a href="/wiki/Phoolan_Devi" title="Phoolan Devi">Phoolan Devi</a> surrendered as he saved her life by instructing the police officers to not kill her in the <a href="/wiki/Police_encounter" class="mw-redirect" title="Police encounter">Police encounter</a> to secure the votes of Dalits (though Phoolan's 22 gang members were killed).<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Singh was an upper caste man and had ruled the vote bank of <a href="/wiki/Uppercaste" class="mw-redirect" title="Uppercaste">upper-caste</a> people in <a href="/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh" title="Uttar Pradesh">Uttar Pradesh</a> for the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> He resumed his post as <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Commerce_and_Industry_(India)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister of Commerce and Industry (India)">Minister of Commerce</a> in 1983.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Leader_of_Rajya_Sabha">Leader of Rajya Sabha</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Leader of Rajya Sabha">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>After he resigned from the position of <a href="/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Uttar_Pradesh" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief Minister of Uttar Pradesh">Chief Minister of Uttar Pradesh</a>, he was appointed as the leader of <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a> in the year 1984 and remained so until 1987. Before him the position was assigned to <a href="/wiki/Pranab_Mukherjee" title="Pranab Mukherjee">Pranab Mukherjee</a>, who was removed because he then formed his own party, <a href="/wiki/Rashtriya_Samajwadi_Congress" title="Rashtriya Samajwadi Congress">Rashtriya Samajwadi Congress</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> After Singh's tenure this position was given to <a href="/wiki/N._D._Tiwari" title="N. D. Tiwari">N. D. Tiwari</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMustafa199578Chand199045–47_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMustafa199578Chand199045–47-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> He resigned from Rajya Sabha when he left Congress in 1987.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERai2006(xviii)_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERai2006(xviii)-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Member_of_Lok_Sabha">Member of Lok Sabha</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Member of Lok Sabha">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>He was elected to Lok Sabha in 1971 from <a href="/wiki/Phulpur_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Phulpur (Lok Sabha constituency)">Phulpur (Lok Sabha constituency)</a>. He lost from <a href="/wiki/Allahabad_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Allahabad (Lok Sabha constituency)">Allahabad</a> in 1977, but won in 1980 as member of Indira Congress. He resigned from Lok Sabha when he became Chief Minister of Uttar Pradesh in June 1980. After he resigned from Congress and quit as Rajya Sabha member in 1987,<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> he entered Lok Sabha by winning the bye-poll for Allahabad seat vacated by <a href="/wiki/Amitabh_Bachchan" title="Amitabh Bachchan">Amitabh Bachchan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> He was elected to Lok Sabha from <a href="/wiki/Fatehpur_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Fatehpur (Lok Sabha constituency)">Fatehpur (Lok Sabha constituency)</a> in 1989 and became Prime Minister for 11 months. He was elected from Fatehpur again in 1991, the last time he contested any election.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Administerial_skill">Administerial skill</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Administerial skill">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>He was considered very close to <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a> as well as <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a> and was loyal to them at a time when the experienced leaders of Congress Party founded a new party, <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress_(Organisation)" title="Indian National Congress (Organisation)">Indian National Congress</a>, and empowered the party of Indira Gandhi (<a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Singh was known as "Mr. Clean" because of his impeccable history and also because of his opposition for the corruption in Bofors deal, which lead the way for him to contest his own party to fight the 1989 Lok Sabha Election and become <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">Prime Minister of India</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> Singh was responsible for managing the coalition of the Left and the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> (BJP) against <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a> to dethrone him in the 1989 elections. He is remembered for the important role that he played in 1989 that changed the course of Indian politics.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Singh acted boldly by issuing an arrest warrant against <a href="/wiki/L._K._Advani" title="L. K. Advani">L. K. Advani</a> midway through the latter's Rath Yatra.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Ministries_under_Central_Government">Ministries under Central Government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Ministries under Central Government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Singh has been on the list as one of the senior-most and most powerful leaders of the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a> and has held many important ministry positions such as <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_(India)" title="Ministry of Defence (India)">Defence</a>, <a href="/wiki/External_Affairs" class="mw-redirect" title="External Affairs">External Affairs</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Finance_(India)" title="Ministry of Finance (India)">Finance</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;B&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Minister_of_Finance_.281984.E2.80.931987.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Minister_of_Finance_(1984–1987)">Minister of Finance (1984–1987)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Minister of Finance (1984–1987)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>He was called to New Delhi following <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi's</a> mandate in the <a href="/wiki/1984_Indian_general_election" title="1984 Indian general election">1984 general election</a>. Singh was appointed to the post of <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Finance_(India)" title="Minister of Finance (India)">Finance Minister</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Tenth_cabinet_of_India_1984" class="mw-redirect" title="Tenth cabinet of India 1984">tenth Cabinet of India</a>, where he oversaw the gradual relaxation of the <a href="/wiki/License_Raj" class="mw-redirect" title="License Raj">License Raj</a> (governmental regulation) as Gandhi had in mind.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> During his term as Finance Minister, he oversaw the reduction of gold smuggling by reducing gold taxes and giving the police a portion of the confiscated gold.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> He also gave extraordinary powers to the <a href="/wiki/Enforcement_Directorate" title="Enforcement Directorate">Enforcement Directorate</a> of the Finance Ministry, the wing of the ministry charged with tracking down tax evaders, then headed by Bhure Lal. Singh's efforts to reduce government regulation of business and to prosecute tax fraud attracted widespread praise.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following a number of high-profile raids on suspected evaders&#160;– including <a href="/wiki/Dhirubhai_Ambani" title="Dhirubhai Ambani">Dhirubhai Ambani</a> and <a href="/wiki/Amitabh_Bachchan" title="Amitabh Bachchan">Amitabh Bachchan</a>&#160;– Gandhi was forced to sack him as Finance Minister, possibly because many of the raids were conducted on industrialists who had supported the Congress financially in the past.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> However, Singh's popularity was at such a pitch that only a sideways move seemed to have been possible, to the Defence Ministry (in January 1987).<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Then he succeeded his position to <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Minister_of_Defence_.281987.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Minister_of_Defence_(1987)">Minister of Defence (1987)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Minister of Defence (1987)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In the year 1987, Singh was appointed on the position of <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_(India)" title="Ministry of Defence (India)">Defence Minister of India</a> for the first time but only for a period less than 3 months from 24 January 1987 to 12 April 1987. He was at that time preceded to <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a> and succeeded in his position to <a href="/wiki/Krishna_Chandra_Pant" class="mw-redirect" title="Krishna Chandra Pant">Krishna Chandra Pant</a>. At that time due to his non-corrupt image, he was also called 'Mr. Clean'.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> He was not able to do any good work for Defence due to holding the position for such a short time. But his biggest work was in the import of <a href="/wiki/Bofors" title="Bofors">Bofors</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Once ensconced in South Block, Singh began to investigate the notoriously murky world of defence procurement. After a while, word began to spread that Singh possessed information about the <a href="/wiki/Bofors_scandal" title="Bofors scandal">Bofors</a> defence deal (the infamous arms-procurement fraud) that could damage Gandhi's reputation.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Before he could act on it, he was dismissed from the Cabinet and, in response, resigned his memberships in the Congress Party (Indira) and the Lok Sabha.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> The deal of Bofors also played a very crucial role in making of his <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">Prime Minister of India</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Minister_of_External_Affairs_.281989.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Minister_of_External_Affairs_(1989)">Minister of External Affairs (1989)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Minister of External Affairs (1989)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>He was appointed as the 16th <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_External_Affairs_(India)" title="Minister of External Affairs (India)">Minister of External Affairs of India</a> and remained in the position for another very short period of just 3 days from 2 December 1989 to 5 December 1989. He was succeeded by <a href="/wiki/Inder_Kumar_Gujral" title="Inder Kumar Gujral">Inder Kumar Gujral</a> for the position.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Formation_of_Janata_Dal">Formation of Janata Dal</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Formation of Janata Dal">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Together with associates <a href="/wiki/Arun_Nehru" title="Arun Nehru">Arun Nehru</a> and <a href="/wiki/Arif_Mohammad_Khan" title="Arif Mohammad Khan">Arif Mohammad Khan</a>, Singh floated an opposition party named <a href="/wiki/Jan_Morcha" title="Jan Morcha">Jan Morcha</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> He was re-elected to Lok Sabha in a tightly contested by-election from Allahabad, defeating Sunil Shastri.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> On 11 October 1988, the birthday of the original <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata</a> coalition's leader <a href="/wiki/Jayaprakash_Narayan" title="Jayaprakash Narayan">Jayaprakash Narayan</a>, Singh founded the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a> by the merger of <a href="/wiki/Jan_Morcha" title="Jan Morcha">Jan Morcha</a>, <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lok_Dal" class="mw-redirect" title="Lok Dal">Lok Dal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress_(Socialist)" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian National Congress (Socialist)">Congress (S)</a>, in order to bring together all the centrist parties opposed to the Rajiv Gandhi government, and Singh was elected the President of the Janata Dal. An opposition coalition of the Janata Dal with regional parties including the <a href="/wiki/Dravida_Munnetra_Kazhagam" title="Dravida Munnetra Kazhagam">Dravida Munnetra Kazhagam</a>, <a href="/wiki/Telugu_Desam_Party" title="Telugu Desam Party">Telugu Desam Party</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Asom_Gana_Parishad" title="Asom Gana Parishad">Asom Gana Parishad</a>, came into being, called the <a href="/wiki/National_Front_(India)" class="mw-redirect" title="National Front (India)">National Front</a>, with V. P. Singh as convener, <a href="/wiki/N._T._Rama_Rao" title="N. T. Rama Rao">NT Rama Rao</a> as president, and <a href="/wiki/P._Upendra" title="P. Upendra">P Upendra</a> as a General Secretary.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The National Front fought <a href="/wiki/1989_Indian_general_election" title="1989 Indian general election">1989 General Elections</a> after coming to an electoral understanding with <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> and the Left parties (the two main oppositions) that served to unify the anti-Congress vote. The National Front, with its allies, earned a simple majority in the Lok Sabha and decided to form a government. The <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janta_Party" class="mw-redirect" title="Bharatiya Janta Party">Bharatiya Janta Party</a> under the leadership of <a href="/wiki/Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee" title="Atal Bihari Vajpayee">Atal Bihari Vajpayee</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lal_Krishna_Advani" class="mw-redirect" title="Lal Krishna Advani">Lal Krishna Advani</a> and the Left parties such as the <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_India_(Marxist)" title="Communist Party of India (Marxist)">Communist Party of India (Marxist)</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_India" title="Communist Party of India">Communist Party of India</a> declined to serve in the government, preferring to support the government from outside.
</p><p>In a meeting in the Central Hall of Parliament on 1 December, Singh proposed the name of <a href="/wiki/Devi_Lal" title="Devi Lal">Devi Lal</a> as Prime Minister, in spite of the fact that he himself had been clearly projected by the anti-Congress forces as the 'clean' alternative to Rajiv Gandhi and their Prime Ministerial candidate. Chaudhary Devi Lal, a <a href="/wiki/Jat_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Jat people">Jat</a> leader from <a href="/wiki/Haryana" title="Haryana">Haryana</a> stood up and refused the nomination, and said that he would prefer to be an 'elder uncle' to the Government and that Singh should be Prime Minister.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> This last part came as a clear surprise to <a href="/wiki/Chandra_Shekhar" title="Chandra Shekhar">Chandra Shekhar</a>, the former head of the erstwhile <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a>, and Singh's greatest rival within the Janata Dal. Shekhar, who had clearly expected that an agreement had been forged with Lal as the consensus candidate, withdrew from the meeting and refused to serve in the Cabinet.<sup id="cite_ref-tribuneindia.com_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tribuneindia.com-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Singh was sworn in as <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">India's Prime Minister</a> on 2 December 1989.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Prime_Minister_.281989_.E2.80.93_1990.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_Minister_(1989_–_1990)">Prime Minister (1989 – 1990)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Prime Minister (1989 – 1990)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Premiership_of_VP_Singh" class="mw-redirect" title="Premiership of VP Singh">Premiership of VP Singh</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/V._P._Singh_ministry" title="V. P. Singh ministry">V. P. Singh ministry</a></div>
<p>Singh held office for slightly less than a year, from 2 December 1989 to 10 November 1990. After state legislative elections in March 1990, Singh's governing coalition achieved control of both houses of India's parliament. During this time, Janata Dal came to power in five Indian states under <a href="/wiki/Om_Prakash_Chautala" title="Om Prakash Chautala">Om Prakash Chautala</a> (<a href="/wiki/Banarsi_Das_Gupta" title="Banarsi Das Gupta">Banarsi Das Gupta</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hukam_Singh_(Haryana_politician)" title="Hukam Singh (Haryana politician)">Hukam Singh</a>), <a href="/wiki/Chimanbhai_Patel" title="Chimanbhai Patel">Chimanbhai Patel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Biju_Patnaik" title="Biju Patnaik">Biju Patnaik</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lalu_Prasad_Yadav" title="Lalu Prasad Yadav">Lalu Prasad Yadav</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Mulayam_Singh_Yadav" title="Mulayam Singh Yadav">Mulayam Singh Yadav</a>, and the National Front constituents in two more <a href="/wiki/N._T._Rama_Rao" title="N. T. Rama Rao">NT Rama Rao</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Prafulla_Kumar_Mahanta" title="Prafulla Kumar Mahanta">Prafulla Kumar Mahanta</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The Janata Dal also shared power in West Bengal under <a href="/wiki/Jyoti_Basu" title="Jyoti Basu">Jyoti Basu</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Kerala" title="Kerala">Kerala</a> under <a href="/wiki/E._K._Nayanar" title="E. K. Nayanar">EK Nayanar</a> and in <a href="/wiki/Rajasthan" title="Rajasthan">Rajasthan</a> under <a href="/wiki/Bhairon_Singh_Shekhawat" title="Bhairon Singh Shekhawat">Bhairon Singh Shekhawat</a> (supporting the Bharatiya Janata Party government from outside). Singh decided to end the Indian army's unsuccessful operation in <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka" title="Sri Lanka">Sri Lanka</a> which <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a>, his predecessor, had sent to combat the Tamil separatist movement.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In <a href="/wiki/Punjab,_India" title="Punjab, India">Punjab</a>, Singh replaced the hard-line <a href="/wiki/Siddhartha_Shankar_Ray" title="Siddhartha Shankar Ray">Siddhartha Shankar Ray</a> as Governor with another former bureaucrat, <a href="/wiki/Nirmal_Kumar_Mukarji" title="Nirmal Kumar Mukarji">Nirmal Kumar Mukarji</a>, who moved forward on a timetable for fresh elections. Singh himself made a much-publicised visit to the <a href="/wiki/Harmandir_Sahib" class="mw-redirect" title="Harmandir Sahib">Golden Temple</a> to ask forgiveness for <a href="/wiki/Operation_Blue_Star" title="Operation Blue Star">Operation Blue Star</a> and the combination of events caused the long rebellion in Punjab to die down markedly in a few months.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He also thwarted the efforts of Pakistan under <a href="/wiki/Benazir_Bhutto" title="Benazir Bhutto">Benazir Bhutto</a> to start a border war with India.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Exodus_of_Kasmiri_Hindus">Exodus of Kasmiri Hindus</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Exodus of Kasmiri Hindus">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Exodus_of_Kashmiri_Hindus" title="Exodus of Kashmiri Hindus">Exodus  of Kashmiri Hindus</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/1989_kidnapping_of_Rubaiya_Sayeed" class="mw-redirect" title="1989 kidnapping of Rubaiya Sayeed">1989 kidnapping of Rubaiya Sayeed</a></div>
<p>V. P. Singh faced his first crisis within few days of taking office, when Kashmiri militants kidnapped the daughter of his Home Minister, <a href="/wiki/Mufti_Mohammad_Sayeed" title="Mufti Mohammad Sayeed">Mufti Mohammad Sayeed</a> (then Chief Minister of <a href="/wiki/Jammu_and_Kashmir_(state)" title="Jammu and Kashmir (state)">Jammu and Kashmir</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-:2_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> His government agreed to the demand for releasing militants in exchange; partly to end the storm of criticism that followed, he shortly thereafter appointed <a href="/wiki/Jagmohan" title="Jagmohan">Jagmohan</a> Malhotra, a former bureaucrat, as Governor of <a href="/wiki/Jammu_and_Kashmir_(state)" title="Jammu and Kashmir (state)">Jammu and Kashmir</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-tribuneindia.com_54-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tribuneindia.com-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;C&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Kashmiri_Hindus" title="Kashmiri Hindus">Hindus</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Kashmir_Valley" title="Kashmir Valley">Kashmir Valley</a>, were forced to flee the Kashmir valley as a result of being targeted by <a href="/wiki/JKLF" class="mw-redirect" title="JKLF">JKLF</a> and Islamist insurgents during late 1989 and early 1990.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> Of the approximately 300,000 to 600,000 Hindus living in the Kashmir Valley in 1990 only 2,000–3,000 remain there in 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> 19 January 1990 is widely remembered by Kashmiri Hindus as the tragic "genocide day" of being forced out of Kashmir.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Before governor <a href="/wiki/Jagmohan_Malhotra" class="mw-redirect" title="Jagmohan Malhotra">Jagmohan</a> took over and the governor's rule was imposed and the army deployed in January 1990, Hindus in the valley, were killed. Of the 2,150 incidents of violence, 2100 attacks were against civilians.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Home_Minister" class="mw-redirect" title="Home Minister">Home Minister</a> at that time <a href="/wiki/Mufti_Mohammad_Sayeed" title="Mufti Mohammad Sayeed">Mufti Mohammad Sayeed</a> was blamed for this act and was called it as the support of the <a href="/wiki/Islamic" class="mw-redirect" title="Islamic">Islamic</a> insurgents to establish <a href="/wiki/Islamic_state" title="Islamic state">Islamic state</a> in <a href="/wiki/Jammu_and_Kashmir_(state)" title="Jammu and Kashmir (state)">Jammu and Kashmir (state)</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;D&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="62_Amendment_of_1989_and_SC-ST_Act">62 Amendment of 1989 and SC-ST Act</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: 62 Amendment of 1989 and SC-ST Act">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Sixty-second_Amendment_of_the_Constitution_of_India" title="Sixty-second Amendment of the Constitution of India">Sixty-second Amendment of the Constitution of India</a> and <a href="/wiki/Scheduled_Caste_and_Scheduled_Tribe_(Prevention_of_Atrocities)_Act,_1989" title="Scheduled Caste and Scheduled Tribe (Prevention of Atrocities) Act, 1989">Scheduled Caste and Scheduled Tribe (Prevention of Atrocities) Act, 1989</a></div><p>In the year 1989, the government by Singh implemented the <a href="/wiki/Scheduled_Caste_and_Scheduled_Tribe_(Prevention_of_Atrocities)_Act,_1989" title="Scheduled Caste and Scheduled Tribe (Prevention of Atrocities) Act, 1989">SC-ST Act of 1989</a> to prevent the atrocities against the members of <a href="/wiki/Scheduled_Castes_and_Scheduled_Tribes" title="Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes">Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> It was enacted when the provisions of the existing laws (such as the Protection of Civil Rights Act 1955 and Indian Penal Code) were found to be inadequate to check these crimes (defined as 'atrocities' in the Act).<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> Recognising the continuing gross indignities and offences against Scheduled Castes and Tribes, the Parliament passed the '<a href="/wiki/Scheduled_Caste_and_Scheduled_Tribe_(Prevention_of_Atrocities)_Act,_1989" title="Scheduled Caste and Scheduled Tribe (Prevention of Atrocities) Act, 1989">Scheduled Castes and Schedule Tribes (Prevention of Atrocities) Act 1989</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> The objectives of the Act clearly emphasised the intention of the government to deliver justice to these communities through proactive efforts to enable them to live in society with dignity and self-esteem and without fear or violence or suppression from the dominant castes. The practice of <a href="/wiki/Untouchability" title="Untouchability">untouchability</a>, in its overt and covert form was made a cognizable and non-compoundable offence, and strict punishment is provided for any such offence. The act was finally passed somehow with controversies.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mandal_Commission_report">Mandal Commission report</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Mandal Commission report">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mandal_Commission" title="Mandal Commission">Mandal Commission</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Mandal_Commission_protests_of_1990" title="Mandal Commission protests of 1990">Mandal Commission protests of 1990</a></div>
<p>Singh himself wished to move forward nationally on social justice-related issues, which would, in addition, consolidate the caste coalition that supported the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a> in northern India, and accordingly decided to implement the recommendations of the <a href="/wiki/Mandal_Commission" title="Mandal Commission">Mandal Commission</a> which suggested that a fixed quota of all jobs in the public sector be <a href="/wiki/Reservation_in_India" title="Reservation in India">reserved</a> for members of the historically disadvantaged called <a href="/wiki/Other_Backward_Classes" class="mw-redirect" title="Other Backward Classes">Other Backward Classes</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;E&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>This decision led to widespread protests among the <a href="/wiki/Upper-caste" class="mw-redirect" title="Upper-caste">upper caste</a> youth in urban areas in northern India. OBC reservation (less creamy layer) was upheld by the Supreme Court in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> Culturally unique features of the protests and riots were <a href="/wiki/Bandh" title="Bandh">bandhs</a> (a version of a strike), <a href="/wiki/Hartal" title="Hartal">hartals</a> (a version of a municipal shut-down), <a href="/wiki/Dharna" class="mw-redirect" title="Dharna">dharnas</a> (a version of swarming).<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Articles also highlighted politicians and victims of rioting during the protests. Although not advisable, late summer travel by airline and vehicle during the protests was possible without delays, between capitals New Delhi and Chandigarh, and Shimla for example. <a href="/wiki/Delhi_Police" title="Delhi Police">Police</a> prevented extending the range and duration of the strikes, and some strike activity from even occurring.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> A national state of emergency was largely not declared to mobilize army units against any one demonstration. The strike helped to give large popularity to the <a href="/wiki/Mandal_Commission" title="Mandal Commission">Mandal Commission</a> report and fueled the political grouping of the <a href="/wiki/OBC_quota" class="mw-redirect" title="OBC quota">OBC</a> castes, which later helped a lot for the strengthening of regional political parties and stronger parties and other than <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Congress</a> and <a href="/wiki/BJP" class="mw-redirect" title="BJP">BJP</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> Due to the loss of the votes of the <a href="/wiki/Backward_caste" class="mw-redirect" title="Backward caste">backward caste</a> neither party opposed it and on seeing the protest nor parties declined it.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Even after the passing of the reservations for the <a href="/wiki/Other_Backward_Class" title="Other Backward Class">Other Backward Class</a>, he was never accepted by them, and his upper-caste voters also didn't have to trust him. <sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChand199087–89_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChand199087–89-87">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:0_46-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> Afterward, OBC leaders flexed their political power and outnumbered upper and lower castes to gain political power in Uttar Pradesh, Bihar, Madhya Pradesh, and Rajasthan. The OBC leaders rejected sharing power with lower caste leaders.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tug_of_war_with_the_Reliance_group">Tug of war with the Reliance group</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Tug of war with the Reliance group">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1990, the government-owned financial institutions like the <a href="/wiki/Life_Insurance_Corporation_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Life Insurance Corporation of India">Life Insurance Corporation of India</a> and the <a href="/wiki/General_Insurance_Corporation_of_India" title="General Insurance Corporation of India">General Insurance Corporation of India</a> stonewalled attempts by the Reliance group to acquire managerial control over <a href="/wiki/Larsen_%26_Toubro" title="Larsen &amp; Toubro">Larsen &amp; Toubro</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> Sensing defeat, the Ambanis resigned from the board of the company. <a href="/wiki/Dhirubhai_Ambani" title="Dhirubhai Ambani">Dhirubhai</a>, who had become Larsen &amp; Toubro's chairman in April 1989, had to quit his position to make way for D. N. Ghosh, former chairman of the <a href="/wiki/State_Bank_of_India" title="State Bank of India">State Bank of India</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Advani_Yatra_1990.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Advani_Yatra_1990.svg/250px-Advani_Yatra_1990.svg.png" decoding="async" width="250" height="285" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Advani_Yatra_1990.svg/375px-Advani_Yatra_1990.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Advani_Yatra_1990.svg/500px-Advani_Yatra_1990.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="808" data-file-height="922" /></a><figcaption>Map of <a href="/wiki/Ram_Rath_Yatra" title="Ram Rath Yatra">Ram Rath Yatra</a> by <a href="/wiki/L._K._Advani" title="L. K. Advani">L. K. Advani</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ram_temple_issue_and_the_fall_of_the_coalition">Ram temple issue and the fall of the coalition</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Ram temple issue and the fall of the coalition">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Ram_Rath_Yatra" title="Ram Rath Yatra">Ram Rath Yatra</a></div>
<p>Meanwhile, the Bharatiya Janata Party was moving its own agenda forward. In particular, the <a href="/wiki/Ram_Janmabhoomi" title="Ram Janmabhoomi">Ram Janmabhoomi</a> agitation, which served as a rallying cry for several Hindu organisations, took on a new life. The party president, <a href="/wiki/L._K._Advani" title="L. K. Advani">LK Advani</a>, with <a href="/wiki/Pramod_Mahajan" title="Pramod Mahajan">Pramod Mahajan</a> as aide, toured the northern states on a <i>rath</i>&#160;– a bus converted to look like a mythical chariot&#160;– with the intention of drumming up support.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Before he could complete the tour by reaching the disputed site in <a href="/wiki/Ayodhya" title="Ayodhya">Ayodhya</a>, he was arrested by <a href="/wiki/Lalu_Prasad_Yadav" title="Lalu Prasad Yadav">Lalu Prasad Yadav</a>'s orders at <a href="/wiki/Samastipur" title="Samastipur">Samastipur</a> on the charges of disturbing the peace and fomenting communal tension. Lalu wanted to prevent the communal clashes which took place at different places for this Rath Yatra, and also Bihar faced a similar scenario in 1989 due to the Shilanyas by Rajiv Gandhi Government. Karsevaks reached the site on 30 October 1990, and by the orders of <a href="/wiki/Mulayam_Singh_Yadav" title="Mulayam Singh Yadav">Mulayam Singh Yadav</a> police fired openly upon the Kar sevaks. A deadly riot took place in Ayodhya on 2 November.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>This led to the Bharatiya Janata Party's suspension of support to the National Front government.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> VP Singh faced the vote of no confidence in the Lok Sabha saying that he occupied the high moral ground, as he stood for secularism, had saved the Babri Masjid at the cost of power and had upheld the fundamental principles which were challenged during the crises. "What kind of India do you want?" he asked of his opponents in Parliament, before losing the vote 142–346;<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> only a portion of the National Front remaining loyal to him and the <a href="/wiki/Communism_in_India" title="Communism in India">Left parties</a> supported him in the vote.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>And then, Singh resigned on 7 November 1990.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;F&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="The_Chandra_Shekhar_government">The Chandra Shekhar government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: The Chandra Shekhar government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"><table class="infobox" style="float: right; clear: right; margin:0 0 1.5em 1.5em"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-size:115%">External video</th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data" style="text-align: left"><span typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="video icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg/16px-Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg.png" decoding="async" width="16" height="16" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg/24px-Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg/32px-Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="128" data-file-height="128" /></span></span> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MUliFLz8s48">The race for PM in Janata Dal and SSP</a>. Retrieved from YouTube on 26 May 2018.</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Chandra Shekhar immediately seized the moment and left the Janata Dal with several of his own supporters (including <a href="/wiki/Devi_Lal" title="Devi Lal">Devi Lal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Janeshwar_Mishra" title="Janeshwar Mishra">Janeshwar Mishra</a>, <a href="/wiki/H._D._Deve_Gowda" title="H. D. Deve Gowda">HD Deve Gowda</a>, <a href="/wiki/Maneka_Gandhi" title="Maneka Gandhi">Maneka Gandhi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ashoke_Kumar_Sen" title="Ashoke Kumar Sen">Ashoke Kumar Sen</a>, <a href="/wiki/Subodh_Kant_Sahay" title="Subodh Kant Sahay">Subodh Kant Sahay</a>, <a href="/wiki/Om_Prakash_Chautala" title="Om Prakash Chautala">Om Prakash Chautala</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hukam_Singh_(Haryana_politician)" title="Hukam Singh (Haryana politician)">Hukam Singh</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chimanbhai_Patel" title="Chimanbhai Patel">Chimanbhai Patel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mulayam_Singh_Yadav" title="Mulayam Singh Yadav">Mulayam Singh Yadav</a>, <a href="/wiki/Yashwant_Sinha" title="Yashwant Sinha">Yashwant Sinha</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vidya_Charan_Shukla" title="Vidya Charan Shukla">VC Shukla</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sanjaya_Sinh" title="Sanjaya Sinh">Sanjay Singh</a>) to form the <a href="/wiki/Samajwadi_Janata_Party_(Rashtriya)" title="Samajwadi Janata Party (Rashtriya)">Samajwadi Janata Party/Janata Dal (Socialist)</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> Although Chandra Shekhar had a mere 64 MPs, <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a> the leader of the Opposition, agreed to support him on the floor of the House; so he won a confidence motion and was sworn in as Prime Minister.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> Eight Janata Dal MPs who voted for this motion were disqualified by the speaker <a href="/wiki/Rabi_Ray" title="Rabi Ray">Rabi Ray</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> His government lasted only a few months before he resigned and called for fresh elections.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-premiership_and_death">Post-premiership and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Post-premiership and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>VP Singh contested the new elections but his party was relegated to the opposition chiefly due to the assassination of Rajiv Gandhi (May 1991) during the election campaign, and he later retired from active politics.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> He spent the next few years touring the country speaking about matters related to issues of social justice and his artistic pursuits, chiefly painting.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Vice_President_of_India,_Shri_Bhairon_Singh_Shekhawat_looking_at_painting_works_by_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_V._P._Singh,_after_inaugurating_the_exhibition,_in_New_Delhi_on_February_14,_2006.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/The_Vice_President_of_India%2C_Shri_Bhairon_Singh_Shekhawat_looking_at_painting_works_by_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_V._P._Singh%2C_after_inaugurating_the_exhibition%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_February_14%2C_2006.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/The_Vice_President_of_India%2C_Shri_Bhairon_Singh_Shekhawat_looking_at_painting_works_by_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_V._P._Singh%2C_after_inaugurating_the_exhibition%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_February_14%2C_2006.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/The_Vice_President_of_India%2C_Shri_Bhairon_Singh_Shekhawat_looking_at_painting_works_by_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_V._P._Singh%2C_after_inaugurating_the_exhibition%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_February_14%2C_2006.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1900" data-file-height="1293" /></a><figcaption>The Vice President of India, Shri <a href="/wiki/Bhairon_Singh_Shekhawat" title="Bhairon Singh Shekhawat">Bhairon Singh Shekhawat</a> looking at painting works by the former Prime Minister Shri V. P. Singh, after inaugurating the exhibition, in New Delhi on 14 February 2006</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1992, Singh was the first to propose the name of the future President <a href="/wiki/K._R._Narayanan" title="K. R. Narayanan">KR Narayanan</a> as a (eventually successful) candidate for vice president.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> Later the same year in December, he led his followers to <a href="/wiki/Ayodhya_dispute" title="Ayodhya dispute">Ayodhya</a> to oppose the Karseva proposed by <a href="/wiki/LK_Advani" class="mw-redirect" title="LK Advani">LK Advani</a>, and was arrested before he could reach the site; the Masjid was demolished by the <a href="/wiki/Kar_Sevak" class="mw-redirect" title="Kar Sevak">Karsevaks</a> a few days later.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> In 1996, the Congress party lost the general elections and Singh was the natural choice of the winning United Front (Singh was one of the forces behind the broad United Front coalition) for the post of Prime Minister. But he declined the offer made to him by communist veteran <a href="/wiki/Jyoti_Basu" title="Jyoti Basu">Jyoti Basu</a>, Bihar strongman <a href="/wiki/Lalu_Prasad_Yadav" title="Lalu Prasad Yadav">Lalu Prasad Yadav</a> and almost all leaders of the Janata family.
</p><p>
In an interview with <a href="/wiki/Shekhar_Gupta" title="Shekhar Gupta">Shekhar Gupta</a> in July 2005, Singh said that he had resigned from the Rajiv Gandhi cabinet due to differences that arose in the dealing of information regarding commissions taken by Indian agents in the <a href="/wiki/Shishumar-class_submarine" title="Shishumar-class submarine">HDW submarine deal</a>, and not due to Bofors.<sup id="cite_ref-vp_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vp-110">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> In April 1987, Singh received a secret telegram from J.C.Ajmani, the <a href="/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_India_to_Germany" title="List of ambassadors of India to Germany">Indian ambassador in West Germany</a>. The telegram stated that Indian agents had received large commissions in the HDW deal. These commissions amounted to a staggering Rs. 32.55 crore (7% of the agreed price). Singh informed Rajiv Gandhi about this and instituted an inquiry. However, the handling of this case led to differences and Singh finally resigned from the cabinet.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup></p><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Prime_Minister,_Dr._Manmohan_Singh,_paying_homage_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister,_Shri_V_P_Singh,_in_New_Delhi_on_November_28,_2008.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh%2C_paying_homage_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_V_P_Singh%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_November_28%2C_2008.jpg/220px-The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh%2C_paying_homage_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_V_P_Singh%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_November_28%2C_2008.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="138" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh%2C_paying_homage_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_V_P_Singh%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_November_28%2C_2008.jpg/330px-The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh%2C_paying_homage_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_V_P_Singh%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_November_28%2C_2008.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh%2C_paying_homage_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_V_P_Singh%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_November_28%2C_2008.jpg/440px-The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh%2C_paying_homage_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_V_P_Singh%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_November_28%2C_2008.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2100" data-file-height="1322" /></a><figcaption>The Prime Minister, Dr. <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a>, paying homage at the mortal remains of the former Prime Minister, Shri V. P. Singh, in New Delhi on 28 November 2008</figcaption></figure>
<p>Singh was diagnosed with cancer in 1998 and ceased public appearances. When his cancer went into remission in 2003, he once again became a visible figure, especially in the many groupings that had inherited the space once occupied by his <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a>. He relaunched the <a href="/wiki/Jan_Morcha" title="Jan Morcha">Jan Morcha</a> in 2006 with actor-turned-politician <a href="/wiki/Raj_Babbar" title="Raj Babbar">Raj Babbar</a> as president.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> After Jan Morcha drew a blank in the 2007 UP elections, Raj Babbar joined the Congress, and Singh's elder son <a href="/wiki/Ajeya_Pratap_Singh" title="Ajeya Pratap Singh">Ajeya Singh</a> took over the reins of the party in anticipation of the 2009 General elections.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> Ajeya Singh then contested as Jan Morcha candidate from Fatehpur, but lost to <a href="/wiki/Rakesh_Sachan" title="Rakesh Sachan">Rakesh Sachan</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Samajwadi_Party" title="Samajwadi Party">Samajwadi Party</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Jan_Morcha" title="Jan Morcha">Jan Morcha</a> was renamed as the National Jan Morcha in June 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> A month later, the Jan Morcha merged with the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> Singh was placed under arrest in <a href="/wiki/Ghaziabad,_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Ghaziabad, India">Ghaziabad</a> as he and his supporters were proceeding towards a hauling where prohibitory orders under <a href="/wiki/Unlawful_assembly" title="Unlawful assembly">Section 144</a> had been imposed to join the farmers agitating against the acquisition of land at <a href="/wiki/Dadri" title="Dadri">Dadri</a> by the <a href="/wiki/Anil_Ambani" title="Anil Ambani">Anil Ambani</a>-owned Reliance Industries and demanding adequate compensation.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> Later, Singh and CPI General Secretary <a href="/wiki/A._B._Bardhan" title="A. B. Bardhan">AB Bardhan</a> were again arrested on the UP border when they were proceeding to Dadri.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> However, Singh and Babbar were later able to evade the police, reaching Dadri on 18 August 2006, and ploughing the land in solidarity with the farmers.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Singh died after a very long struggle with <a href="/wiki/Multiple_myeloma" title="Multiple myeloma">multiple myeloma</a> and kidney failure at <a href="/wiki/Apollo_Hospital" class="mw-redirect" title="Apollo Hospital">Apollo Hospital</a> in Delhi on 27 November 2008, aged 77.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> He was cremated at <a href="/wiki/Allahabad" class="mw-redirect" title="Allahabad">Allahabad</a> on the banks of the River <a href="/wiki/Ganges" title="Ganges">Ganges</a> on 29 November 2008, his son <a href="/wiki/Ajeya_Pratap_Singh" title="Ajeya Pratap Singh">Ajeya Singh</a> lighting the funeral pyre.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> He was cremated with full state honour.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;G&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Office_held">Office held</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Office held">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_Offices">Political Offices</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Political Offices">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<table class="wikitable">

</th>

</th>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Personal life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:D_Roopa_IPS.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/D_Roopa_IPS.jpg/220px-D_Roopa_IPS.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/D_Roopa_IPS.jpg/330px-D_Roopa_IPS.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/D_Roopa_IPS.jpg/440px-D_Roopa_IPS.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1477" data-file-height="963" /></a><figcaption>V. P. Singh and his wife Sita Kumari with <a href="/wiki/National_Cadet_Corps_(India)" title="National Cadet Corps (India)">NCC cadet</a> <a href="/wiki/D._Roopa" title="D. Roopa">D. Roopa</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Singh married Princess Sita Kumari, the daughter of the Raja of Deogarh-Madaria, Rajasthan, on 25 June 1955. It was an arranged marriage. He turned 24 on the day of the marriage, and she was 18. Kumari was a <a href="/wiki/Sisodia" class="mw-redirect" title="Sisodia">Sisodia</a> Rajput descended from <a href="/wiki/Maharana_Pratap" title="Maharana Pratap">Maharana Pratap</a> of <a href="/wiki/Udaipur" title="Udaipur">Udaipur</a>. The couple had two sons, <a href="/wiki/Ajeya_Pratap_Singh" title="Ajeya Pratap Singh">Ajeya Singh</a> (born 1957), a chartered accountant in New York City, and Abhai Singh (born 1958), a doctor at the <a href="/wiki/All_India_Institute_of_Medical_Sciences" class="mw-redirect" title="All India Institute of Medical Sciences">All India Institute of Medical Sciences</a> in New Delhi.<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> After his death, his elder son <a href="/wiki/Ajeya_Pratap_Singh" title="Ajeya Pratap Singh">Ajeya Singh</a> was sworned as the 42nd <a href="/wiki/Raja_Bahadur" class="mw-redirect" title="Raja Bahadur">Raja Bahadur</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Manda_(zamindari)" title="Manda (zamindari)">Manda estate</a> in 2007 and in the year 2009 after two years of Singh's death, he merged his party <a href="/wiki/Jan_Morcha" title="Jan Morcha">Jan Morcha</a> with <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Cultural_legacy">Cultural legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Cultural legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Films">Films</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Films">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ol><li>Juliet Reynolds, an art critic and a close friend of Singh, made a short documentary on him, titled <i>The Art of the Impossible</i> (45 minutes long), and covers his political and artistic career.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Suma_Josson" title="Suma Josson">Suma Josson</a> made another film on Singh titled <i>One More Day to Live</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Shekhar_Gupta" title="Shekhar Gupta">Shekhar Gupta</a>, had interview with Singh in the year 2007, titled <i>Walk The Talk with V. P. Singh.</i><sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup></li></ol>
