<li><a href="/wiki/Manda_(zamindari)" title="Manda (zamindari)">Manda (zamindari)</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jan_Morcha" title="Jan Morcha">Jan Morcha</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/1989_kidnapping_of_Rubaiya_Sayeed" class="mw-redirect" title="1989 kidnapping of Rubaiya Sayeed">1989 kidnapping of Rubaiya Sayeed</a></li></ul></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=V._P._Singh&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-upper-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-11"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-11">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The predecessor state of Manikpur was founded in 1180, by Raja Manik Chand, brother of Raja Jai Chand of Kannauj. Raja Gudan Deo, 16th in descent from Raja Manik Chand, established his capital at Manda in 1542. Raja Ram Pratap Singh was granted the hereditary title of Raja Bahadur by the British Raj in January 1913. The Last Raj Bahadur of Manda, Ram Gopal Singh, adopted a son named Vishwanath Pratap Singh, who became the 7th Prime Minister of India.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-36"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-36">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Vishwanath Singh, was one of the most trusted and noble member of <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a>, under the <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a> holding important ministries in the central government.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-65"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-65">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Representatives of the Jammu and Kashmir Liberation Front telephoned the local newspaper <i><a href="/wiki/Kashmir_Times" title="Kashmir Times">Kashmir Times</a></i> at about 5:30&#160;p.m., stating that their group's <a href="/wiki/Mujahideen" title="Mujahideen">mujahideen</a> had kidnapped Dr Rubaiya Sayeed, and that she would remain their hostage until the government released Sheikh Abdul Hameed, a JKLF "area commander" Ghulam Nabi Butt, younger brother of the convicted and hanged terrorist <a href="/wiki/Maqbool_Butt" class="mw-redirect" title="Maqbool Butt">Maqbool Butt</a>; Noor Muhammad Kalwal; Muhammed Altaf; and <a href="/wiki/Mushtaq_Ahmed_Zargar" title="Mushtaq Ahmed Zargar">Mushtaq Ahmed Zargar</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-71"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-71">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">In order to undermine his political rival <a href="/wiki/Farooq_Abdullah" title="Farooq Abdullah">Farooq Abdullah</a> who at that time was the Chief minister of Jammu and Kashmir, the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Home_Affairs_(India)" title="Minister of Home Affairs (India)">Minister of Home Affairs</a> <a href="/wiki/Mufti_Mohammad_Sayeed" title="Mufti Mohammad Sayeed">Mufti Mohammad Sayeed</a> convinced Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/V.P._Singh" class="mw-redirect" title="V.P. Singh">V.P. Singh</a> to appoint Jagmohan as the governor of the state. Abdullah resented Jagmohan who had been appointed as the governor earlier in April 1984 as well and had recommended Abdullah's dismissal to <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a> in July 1984. Abdullah had earlier declared that he would resign if Jagmohan was made the Governor. However, the Central government went ahead and appointed him as Governor on 19 January 1990. In response, Abdullah resigned on the same day and Jagmohan suggested the dissolution of the State Assembly.<sup id="cite_ref-IndiaToday_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IndiaToday-69">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> The group targeted a Kashmiri Hindu for the first time on 14 September 1989, when they killed Tika Lal Taploo, an advocate and a prominent leader of <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> in Jammu &amp; Kashmir in front of several eyewitnesses. This instilled fear in the Kashmiri Hindus especially as Taploo's killers were never caught which also emboldened the terrorists. The Hindus felt that they were not safe in the valley and could be targeted any time. The killings of Kashmiri Hindus continued that included many of the prominent ones.<sup id="cite_ref-Tikoo_p.414_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Tikoo_p.414-70">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-79"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-79">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Leading to the formation of the Mandal Commission, Indian society was based largely on the principles of Caste and to that extent a partially closed system. The lack of social mobility created a social stratification that played a dominant role within Indian society, laying the context for the Mandal Commission to be formed. Therefore, during the late 1900s, India witnessed caste and class to stand for different patterns of distribution of properties/occupations for individuals. This directly affected Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes that were known collectively as Other Backward Classes (OBC), which were the focus groups that experienced the severities of caste/class stratification within the social organization (caste) found within traditional India.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-100"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-100">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">On November 7, 1990, V.P. Singh resigned after suffering a vote of no confidence by a stunning margin of 356 to 151.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-123"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-123">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">After battling with cancer and renal failure for a decade, former Prime Minister Vishwanath Pratap Singh died on Thursday at New Delhi’s Indraprastha Apollo Hospitals, reports HT Correspondent.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<li id="cite_note-26"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-26">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">In August 1988, V. P. Singh won the bye – election to the <a href="/wiki/Lok_Sabha" title="Lok Sabha">Lok Sabha</a> from <a href="/wiki/Allahabad_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Allahabad (Lok Sabha constituency)">Allahabad</a>, which had been vacated when <a href="/wiki/Amitabh_Bachchan" title="Amitabh Bachchan">Amitabh Bachchan</a>. V. P. Singh won the seat against the Congress( I ) contender, <a href="/wiki/Sunil_Shastri" title="Sunil Shastri">Sunil Shastri</a>, son of the late prime minister, <a href="/wiki/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri" title="Lal Bahadur Shastri">Lal Bahadur Shastri</a>. (<a href="#CITEREFBhargava1990">Bhargava 1990</a>, p.&#160;36)</span>

<li id="cite_note-39"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-39">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite class="citation book cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=5y8mAAAAMAAJ&amp;q=Parliamentary+debates"><i>Parliamentary Debates</i></a>. Lok Sabha Secretariat. 1985. pp.&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=Kk1PAQAAMAAJ&amp;dq=Vp+Singh+as+Minister+of+Commerce&amp;pg=RA1-PA105">105</a>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Parliamentary+Debates&amp;rft.pages=105&amp;rft.pub=Lok+Sabha+Secretariat&amp;rft.date=1985&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%3Fid%3D5y8mAAAAMAAJ%26q%3DParliamentary%2Bdebates&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AV.+P.+Singh" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-40"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-40">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">In May 1985, Singh suddenly removed the import of <a href="/wiki/Terephthalic_acid" title="Terephthalic acid">Purified Terephthalic Acid</a> (PTA) from the Open General License category. As a raw material, this was very important to manufacture polyester filament yarn. This made it very difficult for Reliance Industries under <a href="/wiki/Dhirubhai_Ambani" title="Dhirubhai Ambani">Dhirubhai Ambani</a> to carry on operations. Reliance was able to secure, from various financial institutions, letters of credit that would allow it to import almost one full year’s requirement of PTA on the eve of the issuance of the government notification changing the category under which PTA could be imported.</span>
</li>

(2 March 1987). Retrieved 14 September 2011.</span>

<li id="cite_note-86"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-86">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Double aspects of <a href="/wiki/Mandal_Commission" title="Mandal Commission">Mandal Commission</a>:-

<ul><li>With a coalition of opposition parties, the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a>, and gave outside support to the government led by V. P. Singh. The government did not survive long after V. P. Singh's decision to implement the <a href="/wiki/Mandal_Commission" title="Mandal Commission">Mandal Commission</a>'s recommendations as every party had fear of losing the votes of the upper <a href="/wiki/Caste_system_in_India" title="Caste system in India">castes</a>. (<a href="#CITEREFSinha2013">Sinha 2013</a>, p.&#160;<b>31 (7)</b> (<span style="font-size:85%;">603</span>))</li>
<li>The act was passed finally in 1990, even after a series of <a href="/wiki/Mandal_Commission_protests_of_1990" title="Mandal Commission protests of 1990">protests</a> as it was a bet of the votes of <a href="/wiki/Backward_Castes" class="mw-redirect" title="Backward Castes">backward castes</a>. (<a href="#CITEREFMustafa1995">Mustafa 1995</a>, p.&#160;256)</li></ul>
</span></li>
<li id="cite_note-FOOTNOTEChand199087–89-87"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChand199087–89_87-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="#CITEREFChand1990">Chand 1990</a>, pp.&#160;87–89.<span class="error harv-error" style="display: none; font-size:100%"> sfn error: no target: CITEREFChand1990 (<a href="/wiki/Category:Harv_and_Sfn_template_errors" title="Category:Harv and Sfn template errors">help</a>)</span></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-88"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-88">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Dalits, Subalternity and Social Change in India, p. 21, Ashok K. Pankaj, Ajit K. Pandey, Routledge</span>
